<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the complete Crouzeix conjecture by proving the sharp constant-two numerical-range inequality for every bounded operator on a complex Hilbert space and every matrix-valued polynomial. No separability assumption is needed. The closure of the numerical range is a complete 2-spectral set, and the bound extends to finite matrix-valued functions holomorphic near that closure. For a finite matrix and a bounded convex domain containing its numerical range with regular real-analytic Jordan boundary, the optimal similarity making its conformal disk image contractive is attained with condition number at most two. For the similar matrix, one continuous positive boundary density of mass the identity represents the evaluation of every matrix-valued function holomorphic near the closed domain.

- **claim-conformal-collars** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-conformal-coordinates)): For every admissible $\Omega$ there is a conformal bijection $f:\Omega\to\mathbb D$ such that $f$ and $f^{-1}$ extend univalently through $\overline\Omega$ and $\overline\mathbb D$, respectively. There is also a conformal bijection $G:\{\|t\|>1\}\to\mathbb C\setminus\overline\Omega$ that extends univalently to $\{\|t\|>r\}\cup\{\infty\}$ for some $0<r<1$, as a sphere map, with a simple pole at infinity and $G(\mathbb T)=\partial\Omega$. On $\mathbb T$, $tG'(t)$ is an outward normal and $\psi=f\circ G$ is an orientation-preserving smooth circle diffeomorphism.
  - Explanation: Provides interior and exterior conformal coordinates with univalent extensions across the boundary. The exterior coordinate identifies the outward normal, and the composed boundary coordinate preserves orientation.
- **claim-optimal-contractive-metric** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-structural)): For every admissible $\Omega$, every finite $n\ge1$, every $A\in M_n(\mathbb C)$ with $W(A)\subset\Omega$, and the interior conformal coordinate $f$ above, the problem <a href="#eq:minimum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minimum">[eq:minimum]</a> is strictly feasible and has an attained minimum $1\le\kappa^2\le4$. If $H$ is a minimizing metric, then $$S=H^{1/2},\qquad A'=SAS^{-1},\qquad D=Sf(A)S^{-1}=f(A')$$ satisfy $$D^*D\preceq I,\qquad \rho(D)<1, \qquad \\|S\\|\\|S^{-1}\\|=\kappa\le2.$$
  - Explanation: The conformal image admits an attained optimal positive metric and a similarity whose condition number is at most two. The image is contractive and has spectral radius strictly below one; its norm need not be strictly below one.
- **claim-common-positive-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-representation)): With the same $\Omega,A,f,G$ and any chosen minimizing metric in Theorem <a href="#thm:structural" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:structural">2</a>, there is one continuous function $\Lambda:\mathbb T\to M_n(\mathbb C)$ satisfying $$\Lambda(t)\succeq0,\qquad \int_\mathbb T\Lambda(t)\,d\sigma(t)=I_n,$$ such that, for every positive integer $m$ and every $M_m(\mathbb C)$-valued function $v$ holomorphic on a neighborhood of $\overline\Omega$, $$\label{eq:representation-target} v[A']=\int_\mathbb T\Lambda(t)\otimes v(G(t))\,d\sigma(t), \qquad \\|v[A]\\|\le\kappa\max_{\overline\Omega}\\|v\\|.$$ The same $S$, $\Lambda$, and $\kappa$ work for all $m$ and $v$.
  - Explanation: A fixed minimizing metric supplies a continuous positive boundary density of mass identity. It represents every permitted holomorphic evaluation of the similar matrix, independently of the coefficient size and chosen function.
- **claim-explicit-boundary-density** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-density)): For the fixed minimizing metric, the representation <a href="#eq:representation-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:representation-target">[eq:representation-target]</a> holds, before any bound on $\kappa$ is proved, with $$\begin{aligned} \Phi_D(\zeta)&=(I-D/\zeta)^{-1}+[(I-D/\zeta)^{-1}]^*-I, \label{eq:phi}\\ j(t)&=\frac{tG'(t)f'(G(t))}{\psi(t)}>0, &\Lambda(t)&=j(t)\Phi_D(\psi(t)). \label{eq:lambda} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The disk resolvent gives a positive density. Composing it with the boundary coordinate and including the positive angular Jacobian produces the common density before the optimal condition number is bounded.
- **claim-endpoint-extremal-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-extremal)): If $\kappa>1$, there are $X,Y\in M_n(\mathbb C)$ with $$\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XX^*)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(YY^*)=1,\qquad SX=\kappa X,\qquad SY=Y, \qquad X^*Y=0,$$ and an $M_n$-valued rational function $F_\mathbb D$, holomorphic on a neighborhood of $\overline\mathbb D$ and contractive on $\overline\mathbb D$, such that, for $x=\mathop{\mathrm{vec}}(X)$ and $y=\mathop{\mathrm{vec}}(Y)$, $$F_\mathbb D[D]x=y.$$ In particular $F_\Omega=F_\mathbb D\circ f$ is holomorphic near $\overline\Omega$ and satisfies $F_\Omega[A']x=y$. Its boundary trace $F=F_\Omega\circ G$ is contractive.
  - Explanation: When the optimal metric is nontrivial, separation yields orthogonal endpoint data. A finite unitary realization produces a contractive rational disk function carrying the vectorized upper endpoint to the lower endpoint. Its composition with the interior coordinate is holomorphic near the enclosing domain.
- **claim-exterior-kernel-and-original-resolvent** (construction, [source](#stmt-input-analytic)): Assume the admissible $\Omega$ and the exterior map $G$ supplied above. Write $G(t)=at+b+O(t^{-1})$ at infinity, where $a\ne0$, and $q_G(t)=tG'(t)$. There are scalar polynomials $b_k$, with $b_0=1$, $\deg b_k=k$, and leading coefficient $a^{-k}$, defined by $$\label{eq:import-faber} \frac{q_G(t)}{G(t)-z}=\sum_{k\ge0}b_k(z)t^{-k} \quad\text{at infinity}.$$ There is also the mixed expansion $$\label{eq:import-kernel} B(w,t)=\frac{q_G(t)}{G(t)-G(w)}-\frac{t}{t-w} =\sum_{i,j\ge1}\beta_{ij}w^{-i}t^{-j}, \qquad b_j(G(w))=w^j+\sum_{i\ge1}\beta_{ij}w^{-i}\quad(j\ge1).$$ Here $B$ is holomorphic through the diagonal and both infinities of the exterior product collar. For each $r<\rho<1$ there is a constant $C_\rho$ such that $\|\beta_{ij}\|\le C_\rho\rho^{i+j}$; the series converges normally on the product collar and absolutely uniformly on $\mathbb T^2$. The kernel $$m(w,t)=1+B(w,t)+\overline{B(w,t)}$$ is continuous and nonnegative, with each normalized-circle marginal one.* *For every finite $n$ and every original matrix $A\in M_n(\mathbb C)$ with $W(A)\subset\Omega$, $$\label{eq:import-resolvent} K_A(t)=q_G(t)(G(t)I-A)^{-1} =\sum_{k\ge0}b_k(A)t^{-k},\qquad E_A(t)=K_A(t)+K_A(t)^*\succeq0\quad(t\in\mathbb T).$$ Its coefficients decay geometrically and its series converges absolutely uniformly on $\mathbb T$. The scalar case applies to every fixed $z\in\Omega$.
  - Explanation: Exterior conformal coordinates generate polynomial coefficients and a geometrically decaying mixed expansion. Convexity makes the associated kernel nonnegative with normalized marginals and makes the original matrix resolvent field positive.
- **claim-ordered-cauchy-projection** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-projection)): For finite Laurent data $u$, the polynomial $c_u$ has boundary trace $\mathcal Cu=c_u\circ G$. This map extends to a bounded projection on every $\mathcal H_k$. With $\widetilde\mathcal Cu=(\mathcal C(u^*))^*$, one has $$\mathcal C=P_0+P_++\mathsf MP_+,\qquad \widetilde\mathcal Cu=(\mathcal C(u^*))^*.$$ Its actual Hilbert-space adjoints satisfy $$\label{eq:adjoints} \mathcal C^\dagger u=u_0+u_++\mathsf M^\dagger u_-,\qquad \widetilde\mathcal C^\dagger u=u_0+u_-+\mathsf M^\dagger u_+ =(\mathcal C^\dagger(u^*))^*.$$ All four operators preserve the mean, and $$\label{eq:weighted} \\|\mathcal C^\dagger u\\|_2^2+\\|(\mathcal C^\dagger u)_0\\|_2^2 \le2\\|u\\|_2^2\qquad(u\in\mathcal H_k).$$ If $F=F_\Omega\circ G$ with $F_\Omega$ holomorphic near $\overline\Omega$ and $M_k$-valued, then for every $u\in\mathcal H_k$, $$\label{eq:modules} \mathcal C(F\mathcal Cu)=F\mathcal Cu,\qquad \mathcal C((\mathcal Cu)F)=(\mathcal Cu)F.$$* *For every finite coefficient size and every matrix Laurent polynomial $u$, $$\label{eq:resolvent-contract} \int_\mathbb TE_A\otimes u\,d\sigma =c_u[A]+c_{u^*}[A]^*.$$ In particular, $\int E_A\,d\sigma=2I_n$.
  - Explanation: The physical Cauchy integral of finite Laurent data gives a polynomial whose trace extends to a bounded projection. The actual Hilbert adjoints obey a weighted estimate, both analytic multiplication orders are preserved, and Laurent tests identify the original resolvent evaluation.
- **claim-ordered-density-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison)): Let $k\ge1$, and let $F=F_\Omega\circ G$ be $M_k$-valued, with $F_\Omega$ holomorphic near $\overline\Omega$ and $\\|F(t)\\|\le1$ on $\mathbb T$. Suppose $p,q,r\in\mathcal H_k$ satisfy $$p=p^*,\quad q=q^*,\quad p=F^*r,\quad q=rF^*,\quad r_0=0,\quad\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(p_0)>0.$$ For a positive number $\kappa$, define $$p_e=\mathcal C^\dagger p+\widetilde\mathcal C^\dagger p,\qquad q_e=\mathcal C^\dagger q+\widetilde\mathcal C^\dagger q,\qquad r_e=\kappa\mathcal C^\dagger r+\kappa^{-1}\widetilde\mathcal C^\dagger r.$$ If the $2k$-by-$2k$ block $$\Delta(t)=\begin{pmatrix}p_e(t)&r_e(t)^*\\r_e(t)&q_e(t)\end{pmatrix} \succeq0$$ for almost every $t\in\mathbb T$, then $\kappa\le2$.
  - Explanation: The ordered analytic identities control both diagonal fields by a common nonzero projected cross field. The vanishing matrix mean separates the cross projections into orthogonal Fourier subspaces. Positivity of the resulting block forces the condition number bound.
- **claim-sharp-complete-matrix-polynomial-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-polynomial)): For every pair of positive integers $n,m$, every $A\in M_n(\mathbb C)$, every nonnegative integer $d$, and every choice of matrices $B_0,\ldots,B_d\in M_m(\mathbb C)$, the polynomial $P(z)=\sum_{j=0}^dB_jz^j$ satisfies $$\\|P[A]\\|=\left\\|\sum_{j=0}^dA^j\otimes B_j\right\\| \le2\max_{z\in W(A)}\\|P(z)\\|.$$ The constant is independent of both matrix sizes and the degree. No smaller constant holds uniformly over these choices.
  - Explanation: Every finite matrix polynomial evaluation is bounded by twice its maximum norm on the numerical range. The bound is uniform in both matrix sizes and degree, and the nilpotent example proves uniform sharpness.
- **claim-gaussian-analytic-outer-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian)): For every nonempty compact convex $K\subset\mathbb R^2$ and every $\epsilon>0$, there is an admissible $\Omega_\epsilon$ such that $$K\subset\Omega_\epsilon,\qquad \overline\Omega_\epsilon\subset\{x:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,K)<\epsilon\}.$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian smoothing of distance, with an added strictly convex term, gives admissible outer domains arbitrarily close to a compact convex planar set. The construction covers degenerate numerical ranges as well.
- **claim-bounded-hilbert-space-complete-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-hilbert)): Let $\mathcal H$ be a complex Hilbert space, with no separability assumption, and let $A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal H)$. Suppose first that $\mathcal H\ne\{0\}$, and put $$W(A)=\{\langle x,Ax\rangle:x\in\mathcal H,\ \\|x\\|=1\}, \qquad K=\overline{W(A)}.$$ Then $K$ is compact and convex and contains $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(A)$. For every positive integer $m$, every nonnegative integer $d$, and every matrix polynomial $P(z)=\sum_{k=0}^d B_kz^k$ with $B_k\in M_m(\mathbb C)$, the operator $P[A]=\sum_{k=0}^d A^k\otimes B_k$ on $\mathcal H\otimes\mathbb C^m$ satisfies $$\label{eq:hilbert-polynomial} \\|P[A]\\|\le 2\sup_{z\in W(A)}\\|P(z)\\| =2\max_{z\in K}\\|P(z)\\|.$$ More generally, if $U$ is an open neighborhood of $K$ and $F=(f_{ij}):U\to M_m(\mathbb C)$ is holomorphic, its entrywise holomorphic evaluation, in the same base-first tensor convention, satisfies $$\label{eq:hilbert-holomorphic} F[A]:=\sum_{i,j=1}^m f_{ij}(A)\otimes E_{ij}, \qquad \\|F[A]\\|\le2\max_{z\in K}\\|F(z)\\|.$$ Here $E_{ij}$ are the standard matrix units and $f_{ij}(A)$ is given by the holomorphic functional calculus. In particular, $K$ is a complete $2$-spectral set for $A$: the same estimate holds at every finite coefficient size for every matrix-valued rational function whose poles lie outside $K$, with evaluation by the rational functional calculus. The constant two is optimal uniformly over all these choices.* *If $\mathcal H=\{0\}$, every evaluation is the zero operator. With the supremum over the empty numerical range defined to be zero, the corresponding supremum-norm inequalities are $0\le0$; the compact spectral-set assertion above is stated for nonzero $\mathcal H$.
  - Explanation: Finite compressions transfer the polynomial inequality to bounded operators without a separability assumption. Holomorphic approximation gives the analytic estimate, and rational functions with poles outside the numerical-range closure give the complete spectral-set assertion for nonzero Hilbert spaces. The zero space has a separate zero-evaluation convention.

          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:optimal-similarity}\n \\kappa=\\min\\{\\|R\\|\\|R^{-1}\\|:\n R\\text{ invertible},\\ \\|RTR^{-1}\\|\\le1\\}.$$ The minimum is attained by the positive matrix $S$, with $\\|S\\|\\|S^{-1}\\|=\\kappa$. This identity holds for any fixed finite $T$ with an attained feasible metric problem; the stability assumption above supplies such feasibility. The bound $\\kappa\\le2$ proved below uses the additional conformal and numerical-range hypotheses.",
          "source_id": "PM-325.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-comparison-2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-comparison</summary>\n\n*Completion of Theorems <a href=\"#thm:structural\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:structural\">2</a> and <a href=\"#thm:representation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:representation\">3</a>.* If $\\kappa=1$, the required bound already holds. Otherwise Proposition <a href=\"#prop:extremal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:extremal\">5</a> and Section <a href=\"#sec:bridge\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:bridge\">4</a> supply all the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:comparison\">9</a> at auxiliary size $k=n$: <a href=\"#eq:ordered-densities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ordered-densities\">[eq:ordered-densities]</a> gives both multiplication orders, <a href=\"#eq:matrix-zero\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:matrix-zero\">[eq:matrix-zero]</a> gives the zero matrix mean and positive trace, <a href=\"#eq:positive-block\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:positive-block\">[eq:positive-block]</a> supplies positivity, and <a href=\"#eq:projected-densities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projected-densities\">[eq:projected-densities]</a> identifies its entries. Hence $\\kappa\\le2$. Proposition <a href=\"#prop:density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:density\">4</a> already used this same metric and density for every independently chosen coefficient size $m$, which proves all statements of the two theorems. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-325.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every admissible $\\Omega$, every finite $n\\ge1$, every $A\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$ with $W(A)\\subset\\Omega$, and the interior conformal coordinate $f$ above, the problem <a href=\"#eq:minimum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:minimum\">[eq:minimum]</a> is strictly feasible and has an attained minimum $1\\le\\kappa^2\\le4$.           "source_id": "PM-325.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "With the same $\\Omega,A,f,G$ and any chosen minimizing metric in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:structural\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:structural\">2</a>, there is one continuous function $\\Lambda:\\mathbb T\\to M_n(\\mathbb C)$ satisfying $$\\Lambda(t)\\succeq0,\\qquad \\int_\\mathbb T\\Lambda(t)\\,d\\sigma(t)=I_n,$$ such that, for every positive integer $m$ and every $M_m(\\mathbb C)$-valued function $v$ holomorphic on a neighborhood of $\\overline\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:representation-target}\n v[A']=\\int_\\mathbb T\\Lambda(t)\\otimes v(G(t))\\,d\\sigma(t),\n \\qquad\n \\|v[A]\\|\\le\\kappa\\max_{\\overline\\Omega}\\|v\\|.$$ The same $S$, $\\Lambda$, and $\\kappa$ work for all $m$ and $v$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-extremal",
        "exact_quote": "If $\\kappa>1$, there are $X,Y\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$ with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(XX^*)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(YY^*)=1,\\qquad SX=\\kappa X,\\qquad SY=Y,\n \\qquad X^*Y=0,$$ and an $M_n$-valued rational function $F_\\mathbb D$, holomorphic on a neighborhood of $\\overline\\mathbb D$ and contractive on $\\overline\\mathbb D$, such that, for $x=\\mathop{\\mathrm{vec}}(X)$ and $y=\\mathop{\\mathrm{vec}}(Y)$, $$F_\\mathbb D[D]x=y.$$ In particular $F_\\Omega=F_\\mathbb D\\circ f$ is holomorphic near $\\overline\\Omega$ and satisfies $F_\\Omega[A']x=y$. Its boundary trace $F=F_\\Omega\\circ G$ is contractive.",
        "source_id": "PM-325.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:balance",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:balance}\n XX^*-YY^*=Q-DQD^*.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-325.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:realization-series",
          "exact_quote": "The balance-to-isometry step is the classical interpolation mechanism of [[KatsnelsonKheifetsYuditskii1997](#ref-KatsnelsonKheifetsYuditskii1997), Section 3 of the versioned preprint]; its particular endpoint data and finite-dimensional realization are established here. Put $L=Q^{1/2}$. The rows $R_1=[L\\ Y]$ and $R_2=[DL\\ X]$ have equal products with their adjoints. The map $R_2^*v\\mapsto R_1^*v$ is therefore a well-defined isometry of their ranges. Extend it to a unitary using equal-dimensional orthogonal complements, then take its adjoint. This yields $$[L\\ Y]=[DL\\ X]\n \\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\\\c&d\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n U=\\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\\\c&d\\end{pmatrix}\\text{ unitary}.$$ Thus $L=DLa+Xc$ and $Y=DLb+Xd$. Since $\\|a\\|\\le1$ and $\\rho(D)<1$, iteration gives the norm-convergent identity $$\\label{eq:realization-series}\n Y=Xd+\\sum_{j\\ge0}D^{j+1}Xca^jb.$$ The transfer-function norm identity is the unitary-colligation identity of [[Brodskii1978](#ref-Brodskii1978), Section 3.1, equations (3.1)–(3.4)]. Define, for $|\\zeta|<1$, $$R(\\zeta)=d+\\zeta c(I-\\zeta a)^{-1}b,\n \\qquad F_\\mathbb D(\\zeta)=R(\\zeta)^{\\mathsf T}.$$ For $e\\in\\mathbb C^n$ and $v=(I-\\zeta a)^{-1}be$, unitarity gives $$U\\binom{\\zeta v}{e}=\\binom{v}{R(\\zeta)e},\\qquad\n \\|e\\|^2-\\|R(\\zeta)e\\|^2=(1-|\\zeta|^2)\\|v\\|^2\\ge0.$$ Hence $R$ is contractive. Ordinary transpose preserves its norm and holomorphy. Its rational entries are bounded in $\\mathbb D$, so every apparent pole on $\\mathbb T$ is removable: a genuine pole would diverge on an interior radial approach. After cancellation, finitely many remaining poles stay away from $\\overline\\mathbb D$. This supplies a common neighborhood of that closed disk on which $F_\\mathbb D$ is holomorphic; continuity retains contractivity on the circle.",
          "source_id": "PM-325.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $\\kappa>1$, there are $X,Y\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$ with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(XX^*)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(YY^*)=1,\\qquad SX=\\kappa X,\\qquad SY=Y,\n \\qquad X^*Y=0,$$ and an $M_n$-valued rational function $F_\\mathbb D$, holomorphic on a neighborhood of $\\overline\\mathbb D$ and contractive on $\\overline\\mathbb D$, such that, for $x=\\mathop{\\mathrm{vec}}(X)$ and $y=\\mathop{\\mathrm{vec}}(Y)$, $$F_\\mathbb D[D]x=y.$$ In particular $F_\\Omega=F_\\mathbb D\\circ f$ is holomorphic near $\\overline\\Omega$ and satisfies $F_\\Omega[A']x=y$. Its boundary trace $F=F_\\Omega\\circ G$ is contractive.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-input-analytic",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the admissible $\\Omega$ and the exterior map $G$ supplied above. Write $G(t)=at+b+O(t^{-1})$ at infinity, where $a\\ne0$, and $q_G(t)=tG'(t)$. There are scalar polynomials $b_k$, with $b_0=1$, $\\deg b_k=k$, and leading coefficient $a^{-k}$, defined by $$\\label{eq:import-faber}\n \\frac{q_G(t)}{G(t)-z}=\\sum_{k\\ge0}b_k(z)t^{-k}\n \\quad\\text{at infinity}.$$ There is also the mixed expansion $$\\label{eq:import-kernel}\n B(w,t)=\\frac{q_G(t)}{G(t)-G(w)}-\\frac{t}{t-w}\n       =\\sum_{i,j\\ge1}\\beta_{ij}w^{-i}t^{-j},\n \\qquad\n b_j(G(w))=w^j+\\sum_{i\\ge1}\\beta_{ij}w^{-i}\\quad(j\\ge1).$$ Here $B$ is holomorphic through the diagonal and both infinities of the exterior product collar. For each $r<\\rho<1$ there is a constant $C_\\rho$ such that $|\\beta_{ij}|\\le C_\\rho\\rho^{i+j}$; the series converges normally on the product collar and absolutely uniformly on $\\mathbb T^2$. The kernel $$m(w,t)=1+B(w,t)+\\overline{B(w,t)}$$ is continuous and nonnegative, with each normalized-circle marginal one.*\n\n\n*For every finite $n$ and every original matrix $A\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$ with $W(A)\\subset\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:import-resolvent}\n K_A(t)=q_G(t)(G(t)I-A)^{-1}\n       =\\sum_{k\\ge0}b_k(A)t^{-k},\\qquad\n E_A(t)=K_A(t)+K_A(t)^*\\succeq0\\quad(t\\in\\mathbb T).$$ Its coefficients decay geometrically and its series converges absolutely uniformly on $\\mathbb T$. The scalar case applies to every fixed $z\\in\\Omega$.",

      },

For each $r<\\rho<1$ there is a constant $C_\\rho$ such that $|\\beta_{ij}|\\le C_\\rho\\rho^{i+j}$; the series converges normally on the product collar and absolutely uniformly on $\\mathbb T^2$. The kernel $$m(w,t)=1+B(w,t)+\\overline{B(w,t)}$$ is continuous and nonnegative, with each normalized-circle marginal one.*\n\n\n*For every finite $n$ and every original matrix $A\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$ with $W(A)\\subset\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:import-resolvent}\n K_A(t)=q_G(t)(G(t)I-A)^{-1}\n       =\\sum_{k\\ge0}b_k(A)t^{-k},\\qquad\n E_A(t)=K_A(t)+K_A(t)^*\\succeq0\\quad(t\\in\\mathbb T).$$ Its coefficients decay geometrically and its series converges absolutely uniformly on $\\mathbb T$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-projection",
        "exact_quote": "For finite Laurent data $u$, the polynomial $c_u$ has boundary trace $\\mathcal Cu=c_u\\circ G$. This map extends to a bounded projection on every $\\mathcal H_k$. With $\\widetilde\\mathcal Cu=(\\mathcal C(u^*))^*$, one has $$\\mathcal C=P_0+P_++\\mathsf MP_+,\\qquad\n \\widetilde\\mathcal Cu=(\\mathcal C(u^*))^*.$$ Its actual Hilbert-space adjoints satisfy $$\\label{eq:adjoints}\n \\mathcal C^\\dagger u=u_0+u_++\\mathsf M^\\dagger u_-,\\qquad\n \\widetilde\\mathcal C^\\dagger u=u_0+u_-+\\mathsf M^\\dagger u_+\n                         =(\\mathcal C^\\dagger(u^*))^*.$$ All four operators preserve the mean, and $$\\label{eq:weighted}\n \\|\\mathcal C^\\dagger u\\|_2^2+\\|(\\mathcal C^\\dagger u)_0\\|_2^2\n \\le2\\|u\\|_2^2\\qquad(u\\in\\mathcal H_k).$$ If $F=F_\\Omega\\circ G$ with $F_\\Omega$ holomorphic near $\\overline\\Omega$ and $M_k$-valued, then for every $u\\in\\mathcal H_k$, $$\\label{eq:modules}\n \\mathcal C(F\\mathcal Cu)=F\\mathcal Cu,\\qquad\n \\mathcal C((\\mathcal Cu)F)=(\\mathcal Cu)F.$$*\n\n\n*For every finite coefficient size and every matrix Laurent polynomial $u$, $$\\label{eq:resolvent-contract}\n \\int_\\mathbb TE_A\\otimes u\\,d\\sigma\n =c_u[A]+c_{u^*}[A]^*.$$ In particular, $\\int E_A\\,d\\sigma=2I_n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-325.2"
      },

      "exact_statement": "For finite Laurent data $u$, the polynomial $c_u$ has boundary trace $\\mathcal Cu=c_u\\circ G$. This map extends to a bounded projection on every $\\mathcal H_k$. With $\\widetilde\\mathcal Cu=(\\mathcal C(u^*))^*$, one has $$\\mathcal C=P_0+P_++\\mathsf MP_+,\\qquad\n \\widetilde\\mathcal Cu=(\\mathcal C(u^*))^*.$$ Its actual Hilbert-space adjoints satisfy $$\\label{eq:adjoints}\n \\mathcal C^\\dagger u=u_0+u_++\\mathsf M^\\dagger u_-,\\qquad\n \\widetilde\\mathcal C^\\dagger u=u_0+u_-+\\mathsf M^\\dagger u_+\n                         =(\\mathcal C^\\dagger(u^*))^*.$$ All four operators preserve the mean, and $$\\label{eq:weighted}\n \\|\\mathcal C^\\dagger u\\|_2^2+\\|(\\mathcal C^\\dagger u)_0\\|_2^2\n \\le2\\|u\\|_2^2\\qquad(u\\in\\mathcal H_k).$$ If $F=F_\\Omega\\circ G$ with $F_\\Omega$ holomorphic near $\\overline\\Omega$ and $M_k$-valued, then for every $u\\in\\mathcal H_k$, $$\\label{eq:modules}\n \\mathcal C(F\\mathcal Cu)=F\\mathcal Cu,\\qquad\n \\mathcal C((\\mathcal Cu)F)=(\\mathcal Cu)F.$$*\n\n\n*For every finite coefficient size and every matrix Laurent polynomial $u$, $$\\label{eq:resolvent-contract}\n \\int_\\mathbb TE_A\\otimes u\\,d\\sigma\n =c_u[A]+c_{u^*}[A]^*.$$ In particular, $\\int E_A\\,d\\sigma=2I_n$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "then $\\kappa\\le2$.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:diagonal-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Each $P_j$ has only nonnegative Fourier powers, and its mean is Hermitian. Fourier orthogonality gives $$\\|P_j+P_j^*\\|_2^2\n =4\\|(P_j)_0\\|_2^2+2\\|(P_j)_+\\|_2^2\n =2\\bigl(\\|P_j\\|_2^2+\\|(P_j)_0\\|_2^2\\bigr).$$ Since $\\|F\\|\\le1$, both $F^*g$ and $gF^*$ have Hilbert–Schmidt $L^2$ norm at most $\\|g\\|_2$. Apply <a href=\"#eq:weighted\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted\">[eq:weighted]</a> separately to the two inputs in <a href=\"#eq:ordered-projection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ordered-projection\">[eq:ordered-projection]</a>. Using the star relation in <a href=\"#eq:adjoints\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:adjoints\">[eq:adjoints]</a> and $p=p^*$, $q=q^*$, we obtain $$\\label{eq:diagonal-bound}\n \\|p_e\\|_2^2\\le4\\|g\\|_2^2,\\qquad\n \\|q_e\\|_2^2\\le4\\|g\\|_2^2.$$",

Mean preservation and the whole matrix equality $r_0=0$ put $g$ in the strictly positive Fourier subspace and $h$ in the strictly negative one. Therefore $$\\label{eq:cross-bound}\n \\|r_e\\|_2^2=\\kappa^2\\|g\\|_2^2+\\kappa^{-2}\\|h\\|_2^2\n \\ge\\kappa^2\\|g\\|_2^2.$$ Finally, set $J=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(I_k,-I_k)$. Positivity of both $\\Delta$ and $J\\Delta J$ gives $$0\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}\\bigl(\\Delta^{1/2}(J\\Delta J)\\Delta^{1/2}\\bigr)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(\\Delta J\\Delta J)\n =\\|p_e\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2+\\|q_e\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2-2\\|r_e\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2.$$ The entries are in $L^2$, so this inequality is integrable. Combining it with <a href=\"#eq:diagonal-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:diagonal-bound\">[eq:diagonal-bound]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:cross-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-bound\">[eq:cross-bound]</a> yields $$2\\kappa^2\\|g\\|_2^2\\le2\\|r_e\\|_2^2\n \\le\\|p_e\\|_2^2+\\|q_e\\|_2^2\\le8\\|g\\|_2^2.$$ Since $g\\ne0$, division proves $\\kappa\\le2$. ◻",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible domain** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Call $\Omega$ *admissible* when it is a bounded open convex subset of $\mathbb C$ with regular real-analytic Jordan boundary.
- **Finite-matrix numerical range** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $A\in M_n(\mathbb C)$ its numerical range is $$W(A)=\{x^*Ax:x\in\mathbb C^n,\ x^*x=1\}.$$
- **Matrix-valued holomorphic evaluation** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a matrix-valued function $v$ holomorphic near the spectrum, $v[A]$ denotes holomorphic evaluation: on a Jordan block $J=\beta I+N$ of size $s$ it is $\sum_{j=0}^{s-1}N^j\otimes v^{(j)}(\beta)/j!$, followed by the change of basis on the first factor.
- **Adjoint and transpose conventions** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The symbol ${}^*$ denotes the adjoint on a base or coefficient Hilbert space, ${}^{\mathsf T}$ ordinary transpose, and ${}^\dagger$ the adjoint of an operator on a function space.
- **Optimal positive metric problem** ([source](#eq:minimum)): Put $T=f(A)$ and define $$\label{eq:minimum} \kappa^2=\min\{\tau\in\mathbb R:I\preceq H\preceq\tau I, \ T^*HT\preceq H\text{ for some }H=H^*\}.$$
- **Strictly feasible metric pair** ([source](#eq:minimum)): A strictly feasible pair means that $H-I$, $\tau I-H$, and $H-T^*HT$ are all positive definite.
- **Coefficient-space partial-trace densities** ([source](#eq:partial-traces)): Take the partial trace over the base factor, meaning the sum of the diagonal base-space blocks, leaving a coefficient-space matrix: $$\label{eq:partial-traces} p=\operatorname{Tr}_{\rm base}(x_\Lambda x_\Lambda^*),\quad q=\operatorname{Tr}_{\rm base}(y_\Lambda y_\Lambda^*),\quad r=\operatorname{Tr}_{\rm base}(y_\Lambda x_\Lambda^*).$$
- **Physical Cauchy representative** ([source](#eq:local-bridge)): For continuous matrix-valued boundary data $u$, define the physical Cauchy integral $$c_u(z)=\int_\mathbb Tu(t)\frac{q_G(t)}{G(t)-z}\,d\sigma(t),\qquad z\in\Omega.$$
- **Complete spectral-set assertion** ([source](#stmt-cor-hilbert)): In particular, $K$ is a complete $2$-spectral set for $A$: the same estimate holds at every finite coefficient size for every matrix-valued rational function whose poles lie outside $K$, with evaluation by the rational functional calculus.

| q-common-density-mass | Normalized integral of the common positive boundary density | = | $I_n$ | The density is fixed independently of coefficient size and analytic test function. |

| q-universal-complete-polynomial-constant | Sharp uniform complete polynomial inequality constant | = | $2$ | Uniform over both matrix sizes and polynomial degree. |
| q-gaussian-smoothing-error | Uniform error of Gaussian-smoothed distance | ≤ | $c\delta$ | The Gaussian smoothing parameter is positive; the distance is Lipschitz. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-conformal-coordinate-collars** [claim](#stmt-lem-conformal-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-hilbert-2): Extremal holomorphic mapping, harmonic reflection, finite collar patching, and inversion of the exterior domain. -> Interior and exterior conformal coordinates with univalent collars and a positively oriented boundary map.
- **step-metric-feasibility-attainment** [claim](#stmt-thm-structural) / [proof](#pm-h-7): A convergent Stein series supplies strict feasibility; compactness of bounded feasible pairs supplies attainment; scaling identifies optimal similarity. -> An attained minimizing positive metric and a contractive disk image with strictly smaller spectral radius.
- **step-common-positive-density** [claim](#stmt-prop-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-density): Resolvent congruence proves positivity; uniformly convergent disk moments give evaluation; the boundary Jacobian changes coordinates; an isometric embedding gives compression. -> A continuous normalized positive density representing every permitted coefficient size.
- **step-endpoint-separation-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-extremal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-extremal): Finite-dimensional separation gives a positive dual balance and endpoint supports; equal Gram matrices extend to a unitary realization; transpose reconciles coefficient evaluation. -> Orthogonal endpoint vectors and a contractive rational disk function with exact interpolation.
- **step-exterior-positive-kernel-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-input-analytic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-input-analytic): Reciprocal-coordinate divided differences yield mixed expansions and geometric bounds; supporting half-planes establish kernel and resolvent positivity. -> Exterior polynomial coefficients, a nonnegative kernel with normalized marginals, and a positive original-matrix resolvent field.
- **step-cauchy-projection-ordered-modules** [claim](#stmt-prop-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-projection): Jensen contraction and Fourier exchange determine the projection and actual adjoints; Cauchy evaluation fixes analytic traces; density extends both multiplication orders. -> Weighted adjoint bounds, ordered analytic module identities, and the resolvent testing contract.
- **step-two-density-systems-bridge** [claim](#stmt-thm-structural) / [proof](#sec:bridge): Equality in the common compression gives fiber relations; base-space partial traces preserve coefficient order and the matrix mean; endpoint similarity factors identify projected resolvent densities. -> A positive density block whose diagonals and cross field satisfy the ordered comparison hypotheses.
- **step-ordered-density-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison): Separate adjoints of left and right multiplication control diagonals; zero matrix mean makes cross projections orthogonal; positive block trace comparison bounds the condition number. -> The condition number is at most two.
- **step-complete-structural-representation** [claim](#stmt-thm-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-2): Apply the comparison to the endpoint construction when the metric is nontrivial, then reuse the already constructed density for all target coefficient sizes. -> The sharp optimal metric bound and common positive representation with the same minimizing metric.
- **step-gaussian-outer-domains** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian): Smooth convex distance by Gaussian convolution and add a strictly convex quadratic term; choose a regular sublevel set and identify its boundary as a Jordan curve. -> Admissible domains containing the set within the prescribed distance neighborhood.
- **step-complete-matrix-polynomial-limit** [claim](#stmt-cor-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-2): Finite compression proves numerical-range convexity; analytic outer domains and uniform continuity give the fixed-polynomial limit; a nilpotent matrix proves sharpness. -> The sharp complete polynomial inequality for arbitrary finite matrices, including point and segment numerical ranges.
- **step-bounded-operator-functional-calculus** [claim](#stmt-cor-hilbert) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-hilbert): Compress finitely many vector iterates through the polynomial degree; prove spectral containment using lower bounds for the operator and its adjoint; approximate holomorphic functions on a larger compact neighborhood and pass through a fixed contour. -> Polynomial and holomorphic complete bounds without separability, and the complete spectral-set assertion on nonzero spaces.

Put $L=Q^{1/2}$. The rows $R_1=[L\\ Y]$ and $R_2=[DL\\ X]$ have equal products with their adjoints. The map $R_2^*v\\mapsto R_1^*v$ is therefore a well-defined isometry of their ranges. Extend it to a unitary using equal-dimensional orthogonal complements, then take its adjoint. Since $\\|a\\|\\le1$ and $\\rho(D)<1$, iteration gives the norm-convergent identity $$\\label{eq:realization-series}\n Y=Xd+\\sum_{j\\ge0}D^{j+1}Xca^jb.$$ The transfer-function norm identity is the unitary-colligation identity of [[Brodskii1978](#ref-Brodskii1978), Section 3.1, equations (3.1)–(3.4)]. Define, for $|\\zeta|<1$, $$R(\\zeta)=d+\\zeta c(I-\\zeta a)^{-1}b,\n \\qquad F_\\mathbb D(\\zeta)=R(\\zeta)^{\\mathsf T}.$$ For $e\\in\\mathbb C^n$ and $v=(I-\\zeta a)^{-1}be$, unitarity gives $$U\\binom{\\zeta v}{e}=\\binom{v}{R(\\zeta)e},\\qquad\n \\|e\\|^2-\\|R(\\zeta)e\\|^2=(1-|\\zeta|^2)\\|v\\|^2\\ge0.$$ Hence $R$ is contractive. Ordinary transpose preserves its norm and holomorphy. Its rational entries are bounded in $\\mathbb D$, so every apparent pole on $\\mathbb T$ is removable: a genuine pole would diverge on an interior radial approach.           "exact_quote": "Put $g=\\mathcal C^\\dagger r$, $P_1=\\mathcal C^\\dagger p$, $P_2=\\mathcal C^\\dagger q$.           "exact_quote": "Each $P_j$ has only nonnegative Fourier powers, and its mean is Hermitian. Apply <a href=\"#eq:weighted\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted\">[eq:weighted]</a> separately to the two inputs in <a href=\"#eq:ordered-projection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ordered-projection\">[eq:ordered-projection]</a>. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-optimal-conformal-metric**: The minimizing positive metric attains the optimal similarity condition number for the conformal image. That image is contractive and has spectral radius strictly below one; its operator norm need not be strictly below one.
- **invariant-common-positive-representation**: A fixed minimizing metric supplies a continuous positive boundary density of mass the identity. The same similarity and density represent every permitted matrix-valued holomorphic evaluation, independently of coefficient size and the function being evaluated.
- **invariant-ordered-projection-and-adjoint**: The bounded Cauchy projection preserves analytic multiplication on both sides. Its Hilbert adjoint uses the adjoint of the exterior kernel operator, and the weighted adjoint estimate holds in the Hilbert–Schmidt boundary function space.
- **invariant-ordered-density-comparison**: The comparison requires the entire cross-density matrix mean to vanish, a positive diagonal mean trace, both ordered multiplication identities, and positivity of the projected block. These conditions force the similarity constant to be at most two.
- **invariant-bounded-hilbert-space-extension**: The complete polynomial inequality holds for bounded operators on complex Hilbert spaces without a separability assumption. For a nonzero space, the numerical-range closure is a compact complete spectral set for rational functions with poles outside it, and the stated holomorphic bound also holds.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-strict-norm-contraction**: Require every minimizing conformal image to have operator norm strictly below one, or reject an optimizer with a zero matrix slack. -> The optimizer is allowed to lie on the boundary of the feasible matrix inequalities. Strict spectral-radius control does not imply a strict operator-norm bound.
- **anti-use-unweighted-boundary-change**: Compose the disk density with the exterior boundary parameter while omitting the angular Jacobian. -> The boundary change of variables no longer gives the asserted normalized density and common evaluation identity.
- **anti-use-kernel-as-its-adjoint**: Replace the actual Hilbert adjoint of the exterior kernel operator by the original operator. -> The Fourier exchange and projected-density identities require the adjoint kernel; kernel symmetry is not asserted.
- **anti-use-scalar-cross-mean**: Substitute a vanishing scalar trace for the vanishing cross-density matrix mean. -> A traceless constant matrix can remain in the cross density, so the strictly positive and strictly negative Fourier separation needed for the cross bound is not established.
- **anti-use-similar-matrix-resolvent-positivity**: Justify positivity of the original-resolvent block by assuming numerical-range containment for the similar matrix. -> The positive block is constructed from the resolvent field of the original matrix. Numerical-range containment for the similar matrix is not supplied.
- **anti-use-limiting-intrinsic-density**: Transfer the common similarity and continuous boundary density directly to an arbitrary numerical-range boundary by taking the outer-domain limit. -> The passage to arbitrary numerical ranges takes limits of scalar norm bounds for a fixed polynomial. It does not assert convergence of similarities or densities.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-invariant-compression**: Treat the finite subspace used in the bounded-operator proof as invariant under the original operator for every iterate. -> The construction only preserves the iterates needed for the chosen polynomial and input components. Further iterates need not remain in that subspace.
- **anti-use-singular-holomorphic-input**: Apply the holomorphic or rational bound to a function whose singularities meet the numerical-range closure. -> The required neighborhood of holomorphy is absent, and the stated rational spectral-set regime excludes poles in the closure.
- **anti-use-smaller-universal-constant**: Replace the universal constant two by a smaller constant for all matrices and matrix polynomials. -> The scalar-coefficient nilpotent example attains the stated ratio and excludes a smaller uniform constant.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The general structural and bounded-operator results are not finite executable constructions with a supplied certified input representation and termination contract. The metric proof establishes attainment by compactness: "Feasible pairs below any fixed feasible upper bound form a closed bounded subset of a finite-dimensional space, so a minimum is attained." The conformal construction also uses locally uniform subsequence limits, and the common representation requires a continuous boundary density and integration for arbitrary holomorphic functions. The finite sharpness example can test optimality in that example, but cannot decide these universal assertions or supply the missing conformal and boundary algorithms. No finite oracle is asserted for this panel.

Suppose $p,q,r\\in\\mathcal H_k$ satisfy $$p=p^*,\\quad q=q^*,\\quad p=F^*r,\\quad q=rF^*,\\quad\n r_0=0,\\quad\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(p_0)>0.$$ For a positive number $\\kappa$, define $$p_e=\\mathcal C^\\dagger p+\\widetilde\\mathcal C^\\dagger p,\\qquad\n q_e=\\mathcal C^\\dagger q+\\widetilde\\mathcal C^\\dagger q,\\qquad\n r_e=\\kappa\\mathcal C^\\dagger r+\\kappa^{-1}\\widetilde\\mathcal C^\\dagger r.$$ If the $2k$-by-$2k$ block $$\\Delta(t)=\\begin{pmatrix}p_e(t)&r_e(t)^*\\\\r_e(t)&q_e(t)\\end{pmatrix}\n \\succeq0$$ for almost every $t\\in\\mathbb T$, then $\\kappa\\le2$.",

            "anchor": "stmt-cor-hilbert",
            "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H$ be a complex Hilbert space, with no separability assumption, and let $A\\in\\mathcal B(\\mathcal H)$. Suppose first that $\\mathcal H\\ne\\{0\\}$, and put $$W(A)=\\{\\langle x,Ax\\rangle:x\\in\\mathcal H,\\ \\|x\\|=1\\},\n \\qquad K=\\overline{W(A)}.$$ Then $K$ is compact and convex and contains $\\mathop{\\mathrm{spec}}(A)$. For every positive integer $m$, every nonnegative integer $d$, and every matrix polynomial $P(z)=\\sum_{k=0}^d B_kz^k$ with $B_k\\in M_m(\\mathbb C)$, the operator $P[A]=\\sum_{k=0}^d A^k\\otimes B_k$ on $\\mathcal H\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:hilbert-polynomial}\n \\|P[A]\\|\\le 2\\sup_{z\\in W(A)}\\|P(z)\\|\n             =2\\max_{z\\in K}\\|P(z)\\|.$$ More generally, if $U$ is an open neighborhood of $K$ and $F=(f_{ij}):U\\to M_m(\\mathbb C)$ is holomorphic, its entrywise holomorphic evaluation, in the same base-first tensor convention, satisfies $$\\label{eq:hilbert-holomorphic}\n F[A]:=\\sum_{i,j=1}^m f_{ij}(A)\\otimes E_{ij},\n \\qquad \\|F[A]\\|\\le2\\max_{z\\in K}\\|F(z)\\|.$$ Here $E_{ij}$ are the standard matrix units and $f_{ij}(A)$ is given by the holomorphic functional calculus. In particular, $K$ is a complete $2$-spectral set for $A$: the same estimate holds at every finite coefficient size for every matrix-valued rational function whose poles lie outside $K$, with evaluation by the rational functional calculus. The constant two is optimal uniformly over all these choices.*\n\n*If $\\mathcal H=\\{0\\}$, every evaluation is the zero operator. With the supremum over the empty numerical range defined to be zero, the corresponding supremum-norm inequalities are $0\\le0$; the compact spectral-set assertion above is stated for nonzero $\\mathcal H$.",

            "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-2",
            "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-gaussian</summary>\n\n*Proof of Corollary <a href=\"#cor:polynomial\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:polynomial\">10</a>.* For completeness, the Toeplitz–Hausdorff convexity theorem [[Toeplitz1918](#ref-Toeplitz1918), [Hausdorff1919](#ref-Hausdorff1919)] follows here from two-dimensional compression. Compress to the span of unit vectors realizing any two chosen values. In dimension two, the rank-one orthogonal projections are $$\\frac12\\begin{pmatrix}1+u&v+iw\\\\v-iw&1-u\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad u^2+v^2+w^2=1,\\quad u,v,w\\in\\mathbb R.$$ Taking their trace against the compression is a real-affine map into $\\mathbb C\\simeq\\mathbb R^2$. Its linear part has a nonzero kernel. Each point of the unit ball can be moved in a kernel direction to the sphere without changing its image, so the images of the sphere and ball coincide. That image is convex. The one-dimensional compression case is immediate. Compactness of $W(A)$ follows from compactness of the unit sphere.\n\nApply Lemma <a href=\"#lem:gaussian\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:gaussian\">11</a> to $K=W(A)$. For each fixed polynomial $P(z)=\\sum_{j=0}^dB_jz^j$, with arbitrary $B_j\\in M_m(\\mathbb C)$, Theorems <a href=\"#thm:structural\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:structural\">2</a> and <a href=\"#thm:representation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:representation\">3</a> give $$\\left\\|\\sum_{j=0}^dA^j\\otimes B_j\\right\\|\n \\le2\\max_{\\overline\\Omega_\\epsilon}\\|P(z)\\|.$$ Uniform continuity on a fixed compact neighborhood of $K$ makes the maxima on the right tend to $\\max_K\\|P\\|$. This includes point and segment numerical ranges. It is a limit of scalar bounds for a fixed polynomial; no limiting similarity or boundary density on $K$ is asserted. For sharpness take $n=2$, $m=1$, $P(z)=z$, and $A=\\begin{psmallmatrix}0&2\\\\0&0\\end{psmallmatrix}$. Its norm is two, whereas, for every unit $x\\in\\mathbb C^2$, $$|x^*Ax|=2|x_1x_2|\\le |x_1|^2+|x_2|^2=1,$$ with equality when $x_1=x_2=1/\\sqrt2$. Hence $\\max_{W(A)}|P|=1$, proving optimality uniformly over all finite base sizes, coefficient sizes, and degrees. ◻",

          }

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polynomial evaluation identity supplies a mathematical specification, but the paper does not supply a software implementation, numerical error contract, or executable procedure for computing the numerical-range maximum. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The extension to bounded Hilbert-space operators bounds analytic evaluations. It supplies no simulation dynamics, time discretization, collision model, or accumulated simulation error estimate. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The common positive density provides an exact boundary integral representation that could inform evaluation tools. The paper does not provide quadrature nodes, a stopping rule, or a certified discretization error bound. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The endpoint construction produces a rational disk function through separation, positive factors, and a unitary extension. This is a mathematical construction rather than a specified finite algorithm for obtaining all of its exact input witnesses. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The partial traces define coefficient-space matrix fields for the proof. They do not specify storage, indexing operations, compression guarantees, or a data structure interface. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The boundary projection acts on matrix-valued function spaces and is extended by boundedness and density. No hardware execution model, finite truncation contract, or accelerator resource estimate is provided. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive exterior kernel and original-matrix resolvent establish analytic norm inequalities. They supply no adversary model, cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gaussian expectation is used to smooth a distance function and construct an analytic convex enclosing domain. It supplies no learned model, training objective, inference algorithm, or generalization bound. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | For a supplied admissible domain and its interior conformal coordinate, the paper constructs an optimal contractive similarity by minimizing the scalar upper metric bound subject to Hermitian positive-semidefinite constraints, then taking the positive square root of a minimizing metric. The exact optimization contract is adaptable; computation of the conformal coordinate and certified numerical optimization require additional methods. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite compression transfers polynomial evaluations to bounded operators without an exhausting sequence of subspaces. This establishes a functional-analytic passage, not a distributed protocol, communication contract, or convergence theorem for networked processes. |
| other (operator theory and matrix analysis) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Given the conformal coordinates and a minimizing metric, the paper explicitly constructs a continuous positive boundary density from the disk resolvent and the angular Jacobian. This density represents analytic evaluations at every finite coefficient size using the same similarity. The contract supports mathematical adaptation, while exact construction of the required coordinates and metric remains a prerequisite. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-admissible-enclosing-domain** (hypothesis): The structural similarity and common continuous density require a bounded open convex enclosing domain with regular real-analytic Jordan boundary and numerical-range containment for the original finite matrix. Blocked use: ['Apply the structural representation directly on an unbounded or nonconvex domain.', 'Assume a singular enclosing boundary supplies the conformal collars used in the proof.', 'Claim the optimal conformal similarity bound without establishing numerical-range containment.']
- **limit-stability-does-not-supply-sharp-conformal-bound** (hypothesis): An attained feasible metric problem identifies the optimal similarity condition number for a fixed stable finite matrix. The sharp conformal bound additionally uses the domain and numerical-range hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Transfer the sharp similarity bound to every stable matrix solely from metric feasibility.', 'Treat spectral stability as a substitute for the conformal numerical-range regime.']
- **limit-optimizer-may-have-zero-slacks** (edge_case): Strict feasibility supplies a feasible witness; a minimizing metric may have zero slacks. The similar disk image is contractive with strict spectral-radius control, while its operator norm may attain the contraction boundary. Blocked use: ['Reject a minimizing metric merely because a slack is singular.', 'Infer a strict operator-norm contraction from the strict spectral-radius condition.']
- **limit-conformal-and-metric-existence-not-finite-solver** (nonconstructive): The conformal coordinate is obtained through a locally uniform subsequence limit, and metric attainment is established by compactness. These existence arguments do not supply a finite exact computational contract for arbitrary domains and matrices. Blocked use: ['Claim a terminating exact conformal-coordinate algorithm from the extremal-family proof.', 'Claim an exact optimization procedure or complexity bound from compactness alone.', 'Treat finite numerical agreement as verification of the universal structural theorem.']
- **limit-boundary-density-requires-jacobian-and-holomorphy** (hypothesis): The common density represents evaluation of the similar matrix for functions holomorphic near the closed enclosing domain. Changing from the disk boundary coordinate to the exterior parameter requires the positive angular Jacobian. Blocked use: ['Omit the angular Jacobian while retaining the claimed identity mass.', 'Use the representation for a function singular on the closed enclosing domain.', 'Identify the represented matrix with the original matrix without applying similarity.']
- **limit-continuous-integrals-do-not-certify-quadrature** (approximation): The common representation is a continuous boundary integral identity. A certified finite quadrature rule, discretization error estimate, and stopping criterion are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Replace the boundary integral by a sampled sum and assert the exact representation.', 'Treat a sampled density mass as an exact normalization certificate.', 'Infer an implementation error bound from positivity alone.']
- **limit-endpoint-realization-regime** (hypothesis): The endpoint extremal construction applies when the optimal metric constant exceeds the trivial value. Rationality is asserted for the disk function; its composition with the interior conformal coordinate is required only to be holomorphic near the closed enclosing domain. Blocked use: ['Require the endpoint separation construction in the trivial metric case.', 'Assume the composed domain function is rational.', 'Claim a finite witness-generation algorithm solely from the separation and unitary-extension existence arguments.']
- **limit-original-resolvent-and-normalized-density-distinct** (formal_scope): The positive resolvent block uses numerical-range containment for the original matrix. The resolvent field and the common normalized density have different masses and represent different expressions. Blocked use: ['Assume the similar matrix retains the original numerical-range containment.', 'Substitute the original resolvent field for the normalized common density.', 'Exchange the base-space fields with their coefficient-space partial traces.']
- **limit-cauchy-extension-is-not-boundary-continuation** (formal_scope): Finite Laurent tests have entire polynomial Cauchy representatives. General square-integrable boundary data enter the module identities through bounded extension and density; holomorphic continuation through the boundary is not asserted for such general data. Blocked use: ['Evaluate a general square-integrable Cauchy projection as if a holomorphic neighborhood extension had been supplied.', 'Make a singular boundary substitution in the physical Cauchy integral.', 'Extend the resolvent testing identity beyond its stated Laurent-test regime without a separate argument.']
- **limit-actual-adjoint-and-multiplication-order** (formal_scope): The exterior integral operator and its actual Hilbert adjoint are distinct. The comparison retains separate left and right analytic multiplication identities and does not assume a symmetric kernel. Blocked use: ['Replace the actual adjoint by the original kernel operator.', 'Commute matrix coefficients when applying the ordered module identities.', 'Use a scalar commutation argument as a complete matrix-coefficient proof.']
- **limit-comparison-needs-full-matrix-zero-mean** (hypothesis): The ordered comparison requires a vanishing entire cross-density matrix mean, positive diagonal mean trace, a contractive analytic multiplier, the prescribed ordered identities, a positive metric constant, and an almost-everywhere positive projected block. Blocked use: ['Replace a zero matrix mean by a vanishing scalar trace.', 'Divide by the projected cross norm without establishing that it is nonzero.', 'Infer the metric bound from Fourier orthogonality while omitting block positivity or an ordered identity.']
- **limit-outer-approximation-does-not-limit-density** (approximation): Analytic outer approximation passes scalar norm bounds to an arbitrary finite-matrix numerical range for a fixed polynomial, including point and segment ranges. It does not assert a limiting similarity or boundary density on that compact set. Blocked use: ['Infer an intrinsic continuous boundary density on an arbitrary numerical range.', 'Assume the minimizing similarities converge during outer approximation.', 'Replace the fixed-polynomial limit by an unproved uniform limit over all functions.']
- **limit-gaussian-approximation-input-and-expectation** (hypothesis): The Gaussian outer-domain construction starts with a nonempty compact convex planar set and positive tolerance. Its analytic regularity argument uses the exact Gaussian convolution and a strictly convex added term. Blocked use: ['Apply the stated construction to an empty, noncompact, or nonconvex input set.', 'Replace the Gaussian expectation by empirical samples while asserting the same real-analytic boundary conclusion.', 'Drop the strictly convex term while retaining the supplied regular-level argument.']
- **limit-finite-compression-is-input-specific** (formal_scope): The bounded-operator polynomial proof compresses the finitely many iterates needed for the selected input components through the selected polynomial degree. It does not require or establish an invariant compression subspace for arbitrary further iterates. Blocked use: ['Treat the compression subspace as invariant under the original operator.', 'Reuse the same compression for arbitrary degrees or input vectors without checking the required iterates.', 'Assume an exhausting sequence of subspaces or separability is needed for the transfer.']
- **limit-bounded-complex-hilbert-and-finite-coefficients** (hypothesis): The operator extension concerns bounded operators on complex Hilbert spaces and finite matrix coefficient sizes. It supplies no corresponding assertion for unbounded operators, general Banach spaces, or infinite coefficient spaces. Blocked use: ['Apply the bound directly to an unbounded operator.', 'Transfer the same complete bound to a general Banach-space norm.', 'Replace finite matrix coefficients by arbitrary infinite-dimensional operator coefficients.']
- **limit-zero-space-spectral-set-exception** (edge_case): The compact spectral-set assertion is restricted to nonzero Hilbert spaces. On the zero space every evaluation is zero, and the empty numerical-range supremum uses the separately stated convention. Blocked use: ['Describe the empty numerical-range closure as the compact spectral set asserted for nonzero spaces.', 'Apply a nonempty compact-set argument to the zero-space case.']
- **limit-holomorphic-neighborhood-and-rational-poles** (hypothesis): The holomorphic bound requires an open neighborhood of the numerical-range closure. The rational spectral-set assertion requires poles outside that closure; polynomial approximation is obtained on a larger compact neighborhood so evaluation converges on a fixed contour. Blocked use: ['Apply the holomorphic bound to data defined only on the compact closure.', 'Allow rational poles in the numerical-range closure.', 'Infer convergence of functional-calculus evaluations from approximation on the compact closure alone without the contour argument.']
- **limit-uniform-sharpness-not-equality-for-each-input** (counterexample_scope): The nilpotent scalar-coefficient example proves that no smaller constant works uniformly. This does not assert that every matrix or every polynomial attains the uniform bound. Blocked use: ['Advertise a smaller constant uniformly over the permitted matrices and polynomials.', 'Claim equality in the norm estimate for every input.', 'Use the sharpness example as verification of the full universal theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 325 (`lean/docs/325.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A direct proof of the complete Crouzeix inequality](../../preprints/A-direct-proof-of-the-complete-Crouzeix-inequality-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [The complete Crouzeix theorem: optimal similarity and a common positive boundary representation](../../preprints/The-complete-Crouzeix-theorem-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The complete Crouzeix inequality bounds a matrix-valued polynomial evaluated at a matrix by its maximum norm on the numerical range. The formalized result proves the bound with constant $2$ for every positive matrix and coefficient size, every polynomial degree, and arbitrary complex coefficients. It also proves that no smaller universal constant works. Normality of the matrix and nonempty interior of its numerical range are not assumed.
>
> The formalization proves the complete Crouzeix inequality with sharp constant two for every bounded operator on an arbitrary complex Hilbert space. For every matrix-valued polynomial, its operator evaluation has norm at most twice its supremum norm on the numerical range. The same bound holds for finite matrix-valued functions holomorphic near the closure of the numerical range and for rational functions with poles outside that closure. No separability assumption is required.
>
> For finite matrices in a bounded convex domain with regular real-analytic Jordan boundary, the structural result gives an attained optimal similarity with condition number at most two and one continuous positive boundary density of mass the identity representing every matrix-valued analytic test. The linked supporting statements include numerical-range geometry and finite-compression identities.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

universe u_61 u_63 u_67 u_83 u_84 u_181

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Matrix.Norms.L2Operator ComplexOrder

namespace CompleteCrouzeix



def numericalRange {n : ℕ} (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ) : Set ℂ :=
  {z | ∃ x : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin n), ‖x‖ = 1 ∧
    inner ℂ x (Matrix.toEuclideanCLM (n := Fin n) (𝕜 := ℂ) A x) = z}

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open Set Filter Metric Complex
open scoped Topology


structure DiskCoordinate (U : Set ℂ) (a : ℂ) where
  outer : Set ℂ
  outer_open : IsOpen outer
  closure_subset : closure U ⊆ outer
  toDisk : ℂ → ℂ
  fromDisk : ℂ → ℂ
  analytic_to : AnalyticOnNhd ℂ toDisk outer
  injective_to : InjOn toDisk outer
  noncritical_to : ∀ z ∈ outer, deriv toDisk z ≠ 0
  image_open : IsOpen (toDisk '' outer)
  analytic_from : AnalyticOnNhd ℂ fromDisk (toDisk '' outer)
  inverse_map : MapsTo fromDisk (toDisk '' outer) outer
  left_inverse : ∀ z ∈ outer, fromDisk (toDisk z) = z
  right_inverse : ∀ w ∈ toDisk '' outer, toDisk (fromDisk w) = w
  closedDisk_subset : closedBall 0 1 ⊆ toDisk '' outer
  image_domain : toDisk '' U = ball 0 1
  base_zero : toDisk a = 0

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open Set Filter Metric Complex
open scoped Topology


def exteriorMap (a b : ℂ) (h : ℂ → ℂ) (t : ℂ) : ℂ := a*t+b+h t⁻¹

structure ExteriorCoordinate (U : Set ℂ) where
  radius : ℝ
  radius_gt : 1 < radius
  leading : ℂ
  constant : ℂ
  regular : ℂ → ℂ
  leading_ne : leading ≠ 0
  analytic_regular : AnalyticOnNhd ℂ regular (ball 0 radius)
  injective : InjOn (exteriorMap leading constant regular) {t | radius⁻¹ < ‖t‖}
  noncritical : ∀ t, radius⁻¹ < ‖t‖ → deriv (exteriorMap leading constant regular) t ≠ 0
  boundary_image : exteriorMap leading constant regular '' sphere 0 1 = frontier U
  interior_iff : ∀ t, radius⁻¹ < ‖t‖ →
    (exteriorMap leading constant regular t ∈ U ↔ t ∈ ball 0 1)
  outside : ∀ t, 1 < ‖t‖ → exteriorMap leading constant regular t ∉ closure U

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open Polynomial Finset
variable {A : Type u_61} [Ring A] [Algebra ℂ A]

def scalarJetEval (β : ℂ) (N : A) (s : ℕ) (f : ℂ → ℂ) : A :=
  ∑ i ∈ range s, (iteratedDeriv i f β / (i.factorial : ℂ)) • N ^ i

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open Module Set
open scoped DirectSum
variable {V : Type u_63} [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V] [instFiniteDimensionalℂV : FiniteDimensional ℂ V]

noncomputable def primaryEquiv (T : Module.End ℂ V) :
    V ≃ₗ[ℂ] ⨁ β : ℂ, T.maxGenEigenspace β := by
  classical
  exact (LinearEquiv.ofBijective (DirectSum.coeLinearMap T.maxGenEigenspace)
    (DirectSum.isInternal_submodule_of_iSupIndep_of_iSup_eq_top
      T.independent_maxGenEigenspace T.iSup_maxGenEigenspace_eq_top)).symm

noncomputable def primaryNilpotent (T : Module.End ℂ V) (β : ℂ) :
    Module.End ℂ (T.maxGenEigenspace β) :=
  (T - algebraMap ℂ (Module.End ℂ V) β).restrict
    (fun x hx => T.mapsTo_maxGenEigenspace_of_comm
      (Algebra.mul_sub_algebraMap_commutes T β) β (show x ∈ T.maxGenEigenspace β from hx))

noncomputable def primaryEval (T : Module.End ℂ V) (f : ℂ → ℂ) : Module.End ℂ V :=
  (primaryEquiv T).symm.toLinearMap ∘ₗ
    (DirectSum.lmap fun β => scalarJetEval β (primaryNilpotent T β) (finrank ℂ V) f) ∘ₗ
      (primaryEquiv T).toLinearMap

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open Filter Topology Set Module
variable {n : Type u_67} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]

noncomputable def matrixAnalyticEval (A : Matrix n n ℂ) (f : ℂ → ℂ) : Matrix n n ℂ :=
  Matrix.toLinAlgEquiv'.symm (primaryEval (Matrix.toLinAlgEquiv' A) f)

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open scoped ENNReal NNReal Matrix.Norms.L2Operator MatrixOrder Kronecker
variable {n : Type u_83} {m : Type u_84} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n] [instFintypeM : Fintype m] [instDecidableEqM : DecidableEq m]

def completeAnalyticEval (D : Matrix n n ℂ) (F : ℂ → Matrix m m ℂ) :
    Matrix (n×m) (n×m) ℂ :=
  fun i j => matrixAnalyticEval D (fun z => F z i.2 j.2) i.1 j.1

end CompleteCrouzeix

namespace CompleteCrouzeix
open scoped Matrix MatrixOrder
variable {n : Type u_181} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n] [Nonempty n]

def MetricFeasible (T : Matrix n n ℂ) (τ : ℝ) (H : Matrix n n ℂ) : Prop :=
  1 ≤ H ∧ H ≤ algebraMap ℝ (Matrix n n ℂ) τ ∧ Tᴴ * H * T ≤ H

end CompleteCrouzeix

end

noncomputable section
open Set Filter Metric Complex MeasureTheory
open scoped Matrix Topology ComplexConjugate ComplexOrder MatrixOrder
  Matrix.Norms.L2Operator Kronecker

namespace StructuralCrouzeix
open CompleteCrouzeix

def RegularAnalyticBoundaryAt (U : Set ℂ) (p : ℂ) : Prop :=
  ∃ χ : ℂ → ℂ, χ 0 = p ∧ AnalyticAt ℂ χ 0 ∧ deriv χ 0 ≠ 0 ∧
    (∀ᶠ z : ℂ in 𝓝 0, χ z ∈ frontier U ↔ z.im = 0)

structure IsAdmissible (U : Set ℂ) : Prop where
  isOpen : IsOpen U
  bounded : Bornology.IsBounded U
  convex : Convex ℝ U
  nonempty : U.Nonempty
  jordan : Nonempty (Circle ≃ₜ ↥(frontier U))
  regular : ∀ p ∈ frontier U, RegularAnalyticBoundaryAt U p

def IsMinimizing {n : ℕ} (T : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ)
    (τ : ℝ) (H : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ) : Prop :=
  MetricFeasible T τ H ∧
    ∀ (σ : ℝ) (J : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ),
      MetricFeasible T σ J → τ ≤ σ

def StrictlyFeasible {n : ℕ} (T : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (τ : ℝ) (H : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ), H.IsHermitian ∧
    (H - 1).PosDef ∧
    (algebraMap ℝ (Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ) τ - H).PosDef ∧
    (H - Tᴴ * H * T).PosDef

def analyticSupNorm {m : ℕ} (U : Set ℂ)
    (v : ℂ → Matrix (Fin m) (Fin m) ℂ) : ℝ :=
  sSup ((fun z => ‖v z‖) '' closure U)

def boundaryPoint {U : Set ℂ} (G : ExteriorCoordinate U) (t : UnitAddCircle) : ℂ :=
  exteriorMap G.leading G.constant G.regular (t.toCircle : ℂ)

def FullEndpoint : Prop :=
  ∀ (U : Set ℂ), IsAdmissible U →
    (∀ a ∈ U, Nonempty (DiskCoordinate U a)) ∧
    Nonempty (ExteriorCoordinate U) ∧
    ∀ (a : ℂ), a ∈ U → ∀ (f : DiskCoordinate U a) (G : ExteriorCoordinate U),
      ∀ (n : ℕ), 0 < n → ∀ (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ),
        numericalRange A ⊆ U →
        let T := matrixAnalyticEval A f.toDisk
        StrictlyFeasible T ∧
        ∃ κ : ℝ, 1 ≤ κ ∧ κ ≤ 2 ∧
          (∃ H : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ, IsMinimizing T (κ ^ 2) H) ∧
          ∀ H : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ, IsMinimizing T (κ ^ 2) H →
            let S := CFC.sqrt H
            let A' := S * A * S⁻¹
            let D := S * T * S⁻¹
            H.PosDef ∧ IsUnit S ∧
            D = matrixAnalyticEval A' f.toDisk ∧
            Dᴴ * D ≤ 1 ∧ spectralRadius ℂ D < 1 ∧
            ‖S‖ * ‖S⁻¹‖ = κ ∧
            (∀ R : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ, IsUnit R →
              (R * T * R⁻¹)ᴴ * (R * T * R⁻¹) ≤ 1 →
              κ ≤ ‖R‖ * ‖R⁻¹‖) ∧
            ∃ Λ : C(UnitAddCircle, Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ),
              (∀ t, (Λ t).PosSemidef) ∧
              (∫ t, Λ t ∂AddCircle.haarAddCircle) = 1 ∧
              ∀ (m : ℕ), 0 < m →
                ∀ v : ℂ → Matrix (Fin m) (Fin m) ℂ,
                  AnalyticOnNhd ℂ v (closure U) →
                  completeAnalyticEval A' v =
                    (∫ t, Λ t ⊗ₖ v (boundaryPoint G t) ∂AddCircle.haarAddCircle) ∧
                  ‖completeAnalyticEval A v‖ ≤ κ * analyticSupNorm U v

end StructuralCrouzeix
end

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We resolve the complete Crouzeix conjecture by proving the sharp constant-two numerical-range inequality for every bounded operator on a complex Hilbert space and every matrix-valued polynomial. No separability assumption is needed. The closure of the numerical range is a complete 2-spectral set, and the bound extends to finite matrix-valued functions holomorphic near that closure. For a finite matrix and a bounded convex domain containing its numerical range with regular real-analytic Jordan boundary, the optimal similarity making its conformal disk image contractive is attained with condition number at most two. For the similar matrix, one continuous positive boundary density of mass the identity represents the evaluation of every matrix-valued function holomorphic near the closed domain.
